------------------------------

Prop 9 confirms they were on the roof together, and Lang’s testimony contradicts this by stating Gavelle returned alone.

**Step 2:** Applying spatial propositions: Prop 1, Prop 9, and Prop 10 are relevant. Prop 1 states Gavelle was in front of the elevator with Wang. Prop 9 confirms they were together on the roof. Prop 10 states if the president was inside the Bigg Building, the Bigg Building is in front of the president. This doesn’t directly contradict the elevator scenario, but it highlights the President’s location relative to Gavelle.

**Step 3:** Explicitly stating the proposition(s): Prop 9 and Lang’s testimony directly contradict each other. Prop 9 states Gavelle and Wang were together on the roof. Lang’s testimony claims Gavelle returned alone, suggesting she was the only one who used the elevator. This creates a spatial inconsistency regarding their simultaneous presence on the roof and subsequent elevator usage.

**Step 4:** Spatial reasoning: The contradiction arises from the conflicting accounts of who used the elevator. Lang’s testimony suggests a solitary return for Gavelle, while Prop 9 indicates a shared elevator experience with the President. This spatial disagreement points to a fabricated narrative.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyzing the spatial contradiction:

Ini states she was in the passenger seat of her sister’s car the night of the accident. However, Evidence 2 (Newspaper Clipping 2) states Mimi Miney was driving and fell asleep at the wheel. This creates a spatial contradiction – Ini claims to be in the car, while the newspaper indicates Mimi was driving.

**Step 2:** Checking spatial propositions:

Prop 3 states Mimi Miney was at location Grey Surgical Clinic at the time of the car accident. This directly contradicts Ini’s testimony about being in the car with her. Prop 5 states Lotta Hart can see Maya Fey after taking Photo 1. This doesn’t directly contradict the spatial issue, but it’s relevant to the timeline of events.

**Step 3:** Explicitly stating the applicable proposition:

Prop 3 directly contradicts Ini’s testimony. The newspaper clipping establishes Mimi was driving and fell asleep, while Ini’s statement places her in the passenger seat. This creates a spatial inconsistency regarding who was behind the wheel.

**Step 4:** Explanation of spatial reasoning:

The contradiction arises from conflicting accounts of location and activity at the time of the accident. Ini’s testimony places her in the car, while the evidence points to Mimi being the driver. This difference in spatial positioning is the core of the contradiction.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------

This suggests a shift in the scene’s layout or a deliberate misrepresentation of events. The testimony also mentions Iris’s movement – she was in her room until the murder was discovered, implying she didn’t witness the stabbing.

**Step 2:** Checking spatial propositions:
*   **Prop 1:** Iris was in her room until the murder was discovered, implying she did not witness the stabbing.
*   **Prop 2:** The victim’s staff, with fingerprints on it, blocks the view of the stone lantern, preventing Iris from seeing the attack.
*   **Prop 7:** Iris’s testimony states Elise was facing the stone lantern, preventing Iris from seeing the attack.

**Step 3:** Applying propositions: Proposition 7 directly contradicts Iris’s later claim that Elise was defensive. If Elise was facing the stone lantern, Iris wouldn’t have been able to see the attack, and Elise wouldn’t have been in a defensive position. This creates a spatial inconsistency in Iris’s account. Proposition 1 supports Iris’s claim of not witnessing the stabbing, but doesn’t directly address the contradiction with her later testimony. Proposition 2 is relevant to *why* Iris couldn't have seen the attack, but doesn't directly contradict her narrative of Elise's position.

**Step 4:** Spatial Reasoning: The core of the contradiction lies in the changing description of Elise’s position relative to the stone lantern. Iris initially states she couldn’t see the attack because Elise was facing the lantern, which then becomes a justification for Elise being defensive. This suggests a deliberate attempt to obscure the true sequence of events.
